<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every regular language over a finite alphabet has generalized star height at most four over that same alphabet. We give a complete construction using an affine correction that hides one interval product, a finite clock, and several scales for moving boundaries through periodic words.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every finite alphabet $\Sigma$ and every regular language $L\subseteq\Sigma^*$, one has $h_\Sigma(L)\le4$.
  - Explanation: Every regular language admits a generalized expression of height at most four over its original alphabet.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-split)): Let $D'\subseteq D\subseteq E$, where $E$ is a prefix code, and let $(G_e)_{e\in D}$ be deterministic updates of a finite set $S$. For $x,y\in S$, suppose languages $P_x,Q_y$ satisfy the following conditions.* 1. *If a word in $D^*$ has a prefix $uv$ with $u\in P_x$ and $v\in Q_y$, then its first episode $e$ exists, $uv=e$, and $G_e(x)=y$.* 2. *For every $e\in D\setminus D'$ and every $x\in S$, $e\in P_xQ_{G_e(x)}$.* *Let $W_{x,y}$ be the graph languages for the same updates on $(D')^*$. Then the graph language from $x$ to $y$ on $D^*$ is $$\label{eq:split-expression} \begin{split} D^*\cap\Bigg[W_{x,y}\ \cup\ & \left(\bigcup_{a\in S}W_{x,a}P_a\right) \left(\bigcup_{b,c\in S}Q_bW_{b,c}P_c\right)^*\\[-2pt] &\hspace{22mm}\cdot \left(\bigcup_{f\in S}Q_fW_{f,y}\right)\Bigg]. \end{split}$$ If $D$, all $P_x$, and all $Q_y$ have height at most one, and all $W_{x,y}$ have height at most $b\ge0$, then these new graph languages have height at most $\max\{2,b+1\}$.
  - Explanation: Universal soundness and completeness outside the skipped class yield exact sequence graph languages with a controlled height increase.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-recovery)): Let $U$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $\mathbf F_2$ and let $D$ be a subset of a prefix code. Suppose the update for $e\in D$ is $$G_e(v)=vL_e+\sigma_e,$$ where $L_e$ is linear and $\sigma_e\in U$, both determined by the episode. If $R_{v,z}$ are the graph languages of the affine composite on $D^*$, then the graph language of the product of the linear parts, from $v$ to $y$, is $$\bigcup_{f\in U}\bigl(R_{v,y+f}\cap R_{0,f}\bigr).$$ In particular this recovery does not increase a common height bound.
  - Explanation: Testing the same sequence from the input vector and from zero removes the affine translation through finite Boolean operations.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-line)): Let $U$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over $\mathbf F_2$ and let $\rho$ be a linear right action of $M$ on $U$. There are an integer $l\ge1$ and a function $\beta:M^l\to U$ such that, for every $v\in U$ and every $\mathbf d=(d_1,\ldots,d_l)\in M^l$, some $j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}$ makes the function $$a\longmapsto v\rho(d_1\cdots d_{j-1}a d_{j+1}\cdots d_l) +\beta(d_1,\ldots,d_{j-1},a,d_{j+1},\ldots,d_l)$$ constant on $M$.
  - Explanation: A correction fixed independently of the input makes some coordinate line constant for every state and tuple.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-clock)): Let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty set, let $\mathcal R$ be any finite set, and let $Q\geq 1$ be an integer. Put $$F_{\rm cl}=400, \qquad C_{\rm cl}=2^{\|\Lambda\|}\bigl(1+4F_{\rm cl}^{2}\bigr).$$ There exist a positive integer $n$, all of whose prime factors exceed $Q$, sets $I_\alpha,J_\alpha\subseteq\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z$ for $\alpha\in\Lambda$, a set $W\subseteq\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z$, and residues $v_p\in\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z$ for $p\in\mathcal R$ with the following properties.* 1. *For every $s\in\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z$, the relation $$\mathcal E(s)= \{(\alpha,\alpha')\in\Lambda^2: s\in I_\alpha+J_{\alpha'}\}$$ is either a subset of the diagonal or a singleton consisting of an off-diagonal pair. If $s\notin W$, it is the full diagonal.* 2. *For every $u\in\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z$, $$\#\{(p,p')\in\mathcal R^2:p\ne p',\quad u+v_{p'}-v_p\in W\}\leq C_{\rm cl}.$$* *In particular, $C_{\rm cl}$ depends only on $\|\Lambda\|$.
  - Explanation: A finite cyclic clock isolates mismatched tags and uniformly bounds translated roster ambiguities.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-markers)): Let $d\geq 2$ and $K>3d^2$ be integers. There is a translation-equivariant rule marking integer positions of any two-sided word such that distinct markers are at distance at least $d$. The decision at $z$ uses only $[z-r,z+r)$, for a fixed finite integer $r\geq K$. If there is no marker in the inclusive integer interval $[z-d,z+d]$, then the block $[z,z+K)$ has a positive period less than $d$.
  - Explanation: A local translation-equivariant marker rule separates markers and forces short-period blocks where markers are absent.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-periodic-extension)): A block of length $K>3d^2$ having a positive period below $d$ determines a unique two-sided periodic extension of period below $d$. Two such blocks whose starts differ by less than $K-d^2$ determine the same extension. For the marking rule of Lemma <a href="#lem:markers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:markers">7</a>, if there is no marker in $[z-H,z+H]$ and $H\geq d$, the actual letters on $$[z-H+d,\ z+H-d+K)$$ belong to one such periodic extension.
  - Explanation: Long short-period blocks determine compatible periodic extensions under the stated overlap conditions.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-transport)): Suppose $r$ is usable at $t$, and $h$ is a multiple of $\delta_r$ with $\|h\|\leq R_r$. Write $B_j^{(h)}=B(z_j+h)$. Then $$\label{eq:transport-intervals} T_{B_{j-1},B_j}=T_{B_{j-1}^{(h)},B_j^{(h)}} \qquad(1\leq j\leq l).$$ For a fixed endpoint $b\geq z_l+H$, the last transfer also satisfies $$\label{eq:transport-tail} T_{B_l,b}=T_{B_l^{(h)},b}.$$ These statements apply to finite words whenever the prescribed windows and product intervals are available. They hold for arbitrary finite monoids, without a commutativity assumption.
  - Explanation: Usable shifts preserve ordered interval and tail products without cancellation or commutativity.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-inner-ambiguity)): On any two-sided word and for any integers $t,q$, $$\label{eq:inner-ambiguity} \#\{(p,p')\in\mathcal P_0^2:p\neq p',\ A_0(t+p-p',q)\}\leq C_0.$$ The same bound holds on finite words whenever all the indicated inspection windows are internal.
  - Explanation: A fixed anchor gives a uniform bound on ordered stencil pairs with clock or scale obstructions.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-end-ambiguity)): On a two-sided word, uniformly in $t$, $$\label{eq:end-ambiguity} \#\bigl\{(p,p')\in\mathcal P_1^2:p\ne p',\ \exists\gamma\in\Delta_0\ A_1(t+p-p'+\gamma)\bigr\} \le C_1.$$ The same estimate holds on a finite word whenever all the indicated inspection windows are present.
  - Explanation: Separated ordered differences bound candidate pairs with unstable end-marker searches.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-placement)): The translations of the two buffers and the integers $L,o_e,b_{\rm tail}$ can be chosen so that the following properties hold.* 1. *After $s$, the buffers occur in the order $1,0$, followed by all relevant inner-system windows and cuts, and then by all relevant end-system windows and seeds.* 2. *All bounded windows required at main hypotheses $k-h$, $h\in\mathcal H$, or at aligned hypotheses $k+L-p'$, $p'\in\mathcal P_i$, are internal to every complete episode at a true cut. This includes all usability checks, the additional $\gamma\in\Delta_0$ translates at stage 1, and all shifts in $A_1$.* 3. *A prefix or suffix computation has its required data on its own side of a true cut. Later main data for any guessed role, and later buffer data for any decoded offset, are scheduled after the suffix start.* 4. *Every possible true first-episode end is after all these cuts and bounded control windows.
  - Explanation: Acyclic choices place all mandatory control windows inside complete episodes and on the required side of cuts.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix-code)): The episode language $E$ is a prefix code: no member is a proper prefix of another member. All marker and bounded control decisions associated with an episode are unchanged when letters are appended.
  - Explanation: Internal decision windows and the first mismatch fix an episode endpoint under extension.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-end-guard)): Suppose a finite word begins with a complete episode $e$ of true origin $t$ and anchor $q$. A prefix cut $k$ lies inside this first episode. Let a suffix argument starting at $k$ use an end guard whose candidate list contains $t$ and has diameter at most $w$. If the guard accepts, the suffix ends exactly at the end of $e$, and its reported anchor is $q$. This conclusion holds even if the proposed suffix initially ends before $e$ or extends past $e$ into later letters.* *On the actual suffix of $e$, the guard accepts whenever $A_1(t)$ is false, provided the placement conditions for its list hold.
  - Explanation: Agreement of bounded candidate marker data and a reference exact-end test force the true episode end.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-main-split)): The languages $P_x^{\rm main},Q_y^{\rm main}$ satisfy the two split hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:split" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:split">2</a> for the episode update $g_0$, with $D=E$ and $D'=D_0$.
  - Explanation: The main split remains sound for independent tag choices and complete outside its skipped class.
- **C15** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-table-lift)): There are episode updates $g_{1,e}:U_1\to U_1$ and $g_{2,e}:U_2\to U_2$, affine for every $e\in E$, with the following properties. For $i=0,1$, the update $g_{i,e}$ has a total prefix table $\Phi_i$ at $a_i$. Define a linear map $H_i:U_{i+1}\to U_{i+1}$ on basis vectors by $$[v,c]H_i=[\Phi_i(c)(v),1_M].$$ Writing $\mathbf d_i=(T_{B_{i,0},B_{i,1}},\ldots, T_{B_{i,l_i-1},B_{i,l_i}})$, the next update is $$\label{eq:lift-update} g_{i+1,e}(z)= \left(z\rho_i(T_{s,a_i}) +\beta_i(\mathbf d_i)\rho_i(T_{B_{i,l_i},a_i})\right)H_i.$$ Its linear part $L_{i+1,e}$ satisfies $$\label{eq:lift-linear-part} L_{i+1,e}=\rho_i(T_{s,a_i})H_i, \qquad [v,1_M]L_{i+1,e}=[g_{i,e}(v),1_M] \quad(v\in U_i).$$
  - Explanation: Total tables at every hypothetical prefix product define affine lifts encoding lower updates on distinguished basis vectors.
- **C16** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sequence-lift)): For any $D\subseteq E$ and $i\in\{0,1\}$, graph tests for $g_{i+1}$ on $D^*$ yield graph tests for $g_i$ on $D^*$ by finite Boolean operations. Consequently this recovery does not increase generalized star height.
  - Explanation: Affine recovery on whole sequences recovers lower update graph tests without raising height.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-aligned-splits)): For $i=0,1$, the languages $P_x^{(i)},Q_y^{(i)}$ satisfy the two hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:split" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:split">2</a> for the update $g_{i+1}$, with $D=D_i$ and $D'=D_{i+1}$.
  - Explanation: The residual decoders align independently chosen cuts and satisfy the split hypotheses.
- **C18** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-recursive-recovery)): Three applications of Lemma <a href="#lem:split" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:split">2</a>, with intervening finite Boolean operations, construct tests for the $T$-value on $E^*$ from the component languages defined above.
  - Explanation: Residual splits and Boolean recoveries construct monoid-value tests on episode sequences.
- **C19** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-templates)): Consider a finite collection of templates $$\label{eq:periodic-template} a b^h c,\qquad h\ge0,$$ where $a,b,c\in\Sigma^*$ are fixed for each template and $b\ne\epsilon$. Suppose a test on each template uses only the following data:* 1. *letters in fixed finite collections of windows at offsets from the argument’s start or end, with mandatory availability checks;* 2. *order and availability of endpoints chosen from fixed finite sets of offsets from the start or end;* 3. *$M$-products between these endpoints, whenever available and ordered, and lengths modulo fixed positive integers;* 4. *fixed finite combinations, choices, and calculations from these data.* *The accepted words have a generalized expression of height at most one. Adding or removing a finite language preserves this bound.* *Moreover, any of the exact-end tests of Section <a href="#sec:episodes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:episodes">5</a>, with its reference or decoded origin chosen from finitely many offsets relative to the test start, is covered by a finite language and finitely many templates of the form <a href="#eq:periodic-template" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:periodic-template">[eq:periodic-template]</a>.
  - Explanation: Ultimately periodic exponent tests compile into finite unions of expressions with a single star.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-components)): The episode languages $E,D_0,D_1$ and every suffix component $Q_y$ in Section <a href="#sec:splits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:splits">6</a> have generalized star height at most one. Every prefix component $P_x$ has height zero. These bounds hold over the original alphabet $\Sigma$.
  - Explanation: Bounded prefix lengths and a single continuation search give the required component height bounds over the original alphabet.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-remainder)): For every $d\in M$, the language $F\cap T^{-1}(d)$ has height at most one. Every word admits a unique factorization into a sequence of episodes followed by a word in $F$.
  - Explanation: Incomplete tails have height-one monoid fiber tests and uniquely complete the episode factorization.

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty set, let $\\mathcal R$ be any finite set, and let $Q\\geq 1$ be an integer.         "exact_quote": "Let $d\\geq 2$ and $K>3d^2$ be integers.         "exact_quote": "The translations of the two buffers and the integers $L,o_e,b_{\\rm tail}$ can be chosen so that the following properties hold.*\n\n1.  *After $s$, the buffers occur in the order $1,0$, followed by all relevant inner-system windows and cuts, and then by all relevant end-system windows and seeds.*\n\n2.  *All bounded windows required at main hypotheses $k-h$, $h\\in\\mathcal H$, or at aligned hypotheses $k+L-p'$, $p'\\in\\mathcal P_i$, are internal to every complete episode at a true cut. This includes all usability checks, the additional $\\gamma\\in\\Delta_0$ translates at stage 1, and all shifts in $A_1$.*\n\n3.  *A prefix or suffix computation has its required data on its own side of a true cut.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-table-lift",
        "exact_quote": "There are episode updates $g_{1,e}:U_1\\to U_1$ and $g_{2,e}:U_2\\to U_2$, affine for every $e\\in E$, with the following properties. For $i=0,1$, the update $g_{i,e}$ has a total prefix table $\\Phi_i$ at $a_i$. Define a linear map $H_i:U_{i+1}\\to U_{i+1}$ on basis vectors by $$[v,c]H_i=[\\Phi_i(c)(v),1_M].$$ Writing $\\mathbf d_i=(T_{B_{i,0},B_{i,1}},\\ldots,\n               T_{B_{i,l_i-1},B_{i,l_i}})$, the next update is $$\\label{eq:lift-update}\n g_{i+1,e}(z)=\n \\left(z\\rho_i(T_{s,a_i})\n       +\\beta_i(\\mathbf d_i)\\rho_i(T_{B_{i,l_i},a_i})\\right)H_i.$$ Its linear part $L_{i+1,e}$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:lift-linear-part}\n L_{i+1,e}=\\rho_i(T_{s,a_i})H_i,\n \\qquad\n [v,1_M]L_{i+1,e}=[g_{i,e}(v),1_M]\n \\quad(v\\in U_i).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-134.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-table-lift",
          "exact_quote": "There are episode updates $g_{1,e}:U_1\\to U_1$ and $g_{2,e}:U_2\\to U_2$, affine for every $e\\in E$, with the following properties. Define a linear map $H_i:U_{i+1}\\to U_{i+1}$ on basis vectors by $$[v,c]H_i=[\\Phi_i(c)(v),1_M].$$ Writing $\\mathbf d_i=(T_{B_{i,0},B_{i,1}},\\ldots,\n               T_{B_{i,l_i-1},B_{i,l_i}})$, the next update is $$\\label{eq:lift-update}\n g_{i+1,e}(z)=\n \\left(z\\rho_i(T_{s,a_i})\n       +\\beta_i(\\mathbf d_i)\\rho_i(T_{B_{i,l_i},a_i})\\right)H_i.$$ Its linear part $L_{i+1,e}$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:lift-linear-part}\n L_{i+1,e}=\\rho_i(T_{s,a_i})H_i,\n \\qquad\n [v,1_M]L_{i+1,e}=[g_{i,e}(v),1_M]\n \\quad(v\\in U_i).$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Generalized star height** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The *generalized star height* $h_\Sigma(L)$ is the minimum height of an expression defining $L\subseteq\Sigma^*$.
- **Prefix code** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A *prefix code* is a set $E\subseteq\Sigma^+$ in which no word is a proper prefix of another.
- **Graph language** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A *graph language* from $x$ to $y$ on $D^*$, where $D\subseteq E$, consists of those words in $D^*$ whose successive episode updates send $x$ to $y$.
- **Period of a finite word** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A positive integer $a$ is a *period* of a finite word if its letters at distance $a$ agree whenever both positions belong to the word.
- **Usable scale** ([source](#stmt-def-usable)): A scale $r$ is *usable at the origin $t$* if, at each of its $Z$ centers $z_j$, the following conditions hold: 1. The value $u(z_j+h)$ equals $u(z_j)$ for every integer $h$ with $\|h\|\leq R_r$, including agreement on absence. 2. If $u(z_j)=\bot$, then either $\sigma\leq S_r$, or the inclusive interval $[z_j-R_r,z_j+R_r]$ contains no point of $x+\sigma\mathbb Z$. Here $d,\sigma,x$ refer to the periodic extension at the indicated center. All windows needed by these checks are mandatory.
- **Episode language** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): Let $E$ consist of the nonempty words starting at $s$ for which the required data are available, the anchor rule defines $q$, and the word ends exactly at $q+b_{\rm tail}$. The rule is interpreted using the placement just fixed. Since all tests translate with the letters, it does not depend on an ambient coordinate origin.
- **Remainder language** ([source](#pm-h-30)): Define $$F=\neg(E\Sigma^*),\qquad \Sigma^*=\neg0.$$

| Q9 | R-k_0+1 | = | $k_0$ | Distinct slots differ in at least the specified number of digits. |
| Q10 | C_1 | = | $2\|\Delta_0\|$ | Depends on the first stencil difference set. |
| Q11 | new graph language height | ≤ | $\max\{2,b+1\}$ | Common skipped graph-test height bound. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-split) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-split): Independent-witness splitting -> Exact sequence graph languages
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-affine-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affine-recovery): Compare affine composites from input and zero -> Linear-part graph tests
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-affine-line) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affine-line): Finite dependency estimate and exhaustive finite choice -> Uniform correction hiding an interval product
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-clock) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-clock): Finite coordinate blocks and cyclic transport -> Tag relation and roster ambiguity bound
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-markers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-markers): Finite ordered installation of local markers -> Separated markers and local periodicity
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-periodic-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-periodic-extension): Overlap comparison of short-period extensions -> Compatible periodic continuation
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-transport): Boundary congruences and ordered monoid-power comparisons -> Unchanged interval products
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-inner-ambiguity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-inner-ambiguity): Descending count of digit differences -> First stencil obstruction budget
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-end-ambiguity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-end-ambiguity): Separated differences and marker endpoint crossings -> Second stencil obstruction budget
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-placement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-placement): Successive finite envelope placement -> Available data on the correct side of each cut
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-end-guard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-end-guard): Bounded marker agreement and reference mismatch search -> Exact consumption of the true episode
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-prop-main-split) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-main-split): Independent tag soundness and usable-scale completeness -> Main split interface
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-table-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-table-lift): Total hypothetical-prefix tables and basis encoding -> Affine lifts of lower episode updates
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-lem-sequence-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sequence-lift): Distinguished-basis queries on complete sequences -> Lower update graph languages
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-prop-aligned-splits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-aligned-splits): Distinct residual origin decoders and lost-offset counts -> Residual split interfaces
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-prop-recursive-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-recursive-recovery): Residual splits followed by main split and Boolean recoveries -> Monoid-value tests on episode sequences
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-lem-templates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-templates): Ultimately periodic exponent classes -> Height-one expressions over the original alphabet
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-prop-components) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-components): Standalone suffix templates and bounded prefix lengths -> Required component height bounds
- **R19** [claim](#stmt-lem-remainder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-remainder): Incomplete-tail templates and prefix-code parsing -> Remainder fibers and unique factorization
- **R20** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-remainder-2): Split height recurrence and union of episode-remainder products -> Uniform height-four expression

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-affine**: Recover the linear composite by comparing its affine outputs at the input vector and at zero on the same episode sequence.
- **I-uniform**: Choose the affine correction before selecting an input state or tuple; only the successful coordinate may depend on them.
- **I-windows**: Retain every candidate and reject when any mandatory control window is missing.
- **I-alphabet**: Compile component expressions directly over the given alphabet and keep tags as finite test indices.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-size**: Use the height bound as a practical bound on generated expression size or search time. -> Finite existence and bounded nesting do not supply a useful size or running-time bound.
- **A-words**: Recover a linear action using affine tests on different words. -> The episode-dependent translations need not cancel across different sequences.
- **A-morphisms**: Transfer the generalized-height bound through an arbitrary inverse morphism without proving preservation. -> The cited alphabetic preservation statement does not cover arbitrary morphisms.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Finite affine recovery is executable once episode updates are supplied. This panel checks its algebraic kernel, not the universal height bound, episode geometry or expression compilation.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON dimension d and sequence length k; exhaustively enumerate affine maps.
# PRECONDITIONS: integer 0 <= d <= 2 and integer 0 <= k <= 2.
# OUTPUTS: affine and recovered linear truth tables for every ordered map sequence.
# TIME: O(A^k * (k*d^3 + 2^d*d^2)), A = 2^(d*d+d).
# SPACE: O(A^k * 2^d + A*d^2) including the emitted tables.
# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpz arithmetic reduced modulo 2.
# FAILURE: malformed JSON or inputs outside the finite contract exit nonzero.
import sys, json
from itertools import product
from flint import fmpz

def main():
    payload = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
    d, k = payload['dimension'], payload['length']
    if type(d) is not int or type(k) is not int or not (0 <= d <= 2 and 0 <= k <= 2):
        raise ValueError('outside admitted domain')
    maps = []
    for encoding in range(1 << (d*d+d)):
        matrix = [[fmpz((encoding >> (i*d+j)) & 1) for j in range(d)] for i in range(d)]
        offset = [fmpz((encoding >> (d*d+j)) & 1) for j in range(d)]
        maps.append((matrix, offset))
    rows = []
    for seq in product(range(len(maps)), repeat=k):
        linear = [[fmpz(i == j) for j in range(d)] for i in range(d)]
        constant = [fmpz(0) for j in range(d)]
        for idx in seq:
            matrix, offset = maps[idx]
            linear = [[sum(linear[i][a]*matrix[a][j] for a in range(d)) % 2 for j in range(d)] for i in range(d)]
            constant = [(sum(constant[a]*matrix[a][j] for a in range(d)) + offset[j]) % 2 for j in range(d)]
        zero = sum(int(constant[j]) << j for j in range(d))
        affine, recovered = [], []
        for state in range(1 << d):
            image = sum(int((sum(((state >> i) & 1)*linear[i][j] for i in range(d)) + constant[j]) % 2) << j for j in range(d))
            affine.append(image)
            recovered.append(image ^ zero)
        rows.append([affine, recovered])
    print(json.dumps(rows, separators=(',', ':')))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **finite_regular_scope** (hypothesis): The uniform bound concerns regular languages over a fixed finite alphabet. Blocked use: ['Extending the theorem to nonregular languages or infinite alphabets.']
- **boolean_expression_model** (formal_scope): The height bound uses complement in the same free monoid together with union, concatenation, and Kleene star. Blocked use: ['Transferring the bound to expressions without complement.']
- **no_size_bound** (formal_scope): The construction makes effective finite choices but provides no useful size or expression-length bound. Blocked use: ['Treating bounded star nesting as a practical expression-size guarantee.']
- **height_one_unresolved** (unknown): The theorem does not establish whether height one always suffices or exhibit a language requiring greater height. Blocked use: ['Reading the upper bound as a lower bound or a resolution of the height-one question.']
- **original_alphabet** (formal_scope): Finite tags index tests and remain separate from input letters; compilation is over the given alphabet. Blocked use: ['Replacing the original alphabet by auxiliary tags and claiming the same result without compilation.']
- **uniform_affine_choice** (hypothesis): The correction function is fixed before the input state and tuple; only the successful coordinate may depend on them. Blocked use: ['Choosing a different correction after each input state or tuple is observed.']
- **affine_search_cost** (formal_scope): Finite enumeration supplies an existence procedure, without a useful running-time bound. Blocked use: ['Claiming an efficient affine-correction synthesis algorithm.']
- **mandatory_windows** (hypothesis): Unavailable windows cause rejection and cannot remove candidates from universal or uniqueness tests. Blocked use: ['Accepting truncated suffix arguments after discarding candidates with missing data.']
- **independent_split_soundness** (hypothesis): Split factors choose finite information independently, so correctness must cover every accepted pair and incorrect proposed ends. Blocked use: ['Checking only matching guesses or intended complete suffixes.']
- **transport_preconditions** (hypothesis): Boundary transport requires a usable scale and a displacement that is a bounded multiple of its step. Blocked use: ['Moving boundaries through periodic regions without the scale-usability and displacement conditions.']
- **template_restriction** (hypothesis): Height-one compilation uses a single unbounded periodic continuation with ultimately periodic exponent tests. Blocked use: ['Inferring height one solely from regularity of a component language.']
- **empty_alphabet** (edge_case): For the empty alphabet the only languages are the empty language and the singleton empty word, both of height zero. Blocked use: ['Assuming a nonempty alphabet is necessary for the main theorem.']
- **finite_kernel_scope** (formal_scope): A finite test of affine recovery addresses that algebraic identity on the checked inputs; it does not prove the full language construction. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite affine-recovery test as certification of the universal generalized star-height theorem.']

> ## Scope
>
> Generalized star height measures the nesting of Kleene stars in regular expressions that also allow Boolean operations. The formalization proves that every regular language over a finite alphabet has a generalized expression over the same alphabet of star height at most three. This is stronger than the bound of thirteen in the accompanying finite-monoid paper. The selected statement asserts the uniform expression bound, without separately encoding every construction step of that paper.
>
> The formalization proves that every regular language over a finite alphabet has a generalized regular expression over that same alphabet with star height at most three. Generalized expressions allow Boolean operations as well as concatenation and Kleene star. The bound of three implies the accompanying paper's bound of four; the selected statement concerns the expression bound itself.
>
> Generalized star height measures the nesting of Kleene stars in regular expressions that also allow Boolean operations. The formalization proves that every regular language over a finite alphabet has a generalized regular expression over that same alphabet of star height at most three. Complements are taken in the same free monoid. This is the paper's uniform bound.

Comparator source `GeneralizedStarHeight.lean` (1368 bytes, sha256 `795709e34771f1c786ecae7c123e8a586c426d8e93c8b5bc82f2904d5340556e`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace GeneralizedStarHeight

universe u

inductive Expression (Alphabet : Type u) : Type u
  | zero : Expression Alphabet
  | one : Expression Alphabet
  | letter : Alphabet → Expression Alphabet
  | union : Expression Alphabet → Expression Alphabet → Expression Alphabet
  | concat : Expression Alphabet → Expression Alphabet → Expression Alphabet
  | compl : Expression Alphabet → Expression Alphabet
  | star : Expression Alphabet → Expression Alphabet

namespace Expression

open scoped Computability

def language {Alphabet : Type u} : Expression Alphabet → Language Alphabet
  | .zero => 0
  | .one => 1
  | .letter a => {[a]}
  | .union P Q => P.language + Q.language
  | .concat P Q => P.language * Q.language
  | .compl P => P.languageᶜ
  | .star P => P.language∗

def height {Alphabet : Type u} : Expression Alphabet → ℕ
  | .zero | .one | .letter _ => 0
  | .union P Q | .concat P Q => max P.height Q.height
  | .compl P => P.height
  | .star P => 1 + P.height

end Expression

def HasHeightAtMost {Alphabet : Type u} (L : Language Alphabet) (n : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ P : Expression Alphabet, P.language = L ∧ P.height ≤ n

theorem main {Alphabet : Type u} [Finite Alphabet] (L : Language Alphabet) (hL : L.IsRegular) :
    HasHeightAtMost L 3 := by
  sorry

end GeneralizedStarHeight

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every regular language over a finite alphabet has generalized star height at most four over that same alphabet. We give a complete construction using an affine correction that hides one interval product, a finite clock, and several scales for moving boundaries through periodic words.
